I don’t really see her… it’s like she’s by the kitchen window, clear enough like she’s standing there, telling me to check the locks or move his pills.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voice you hear is coming from inside your head or from outside?\n\nPatient: It feels like it’s outside… near the kitchen window. Not in my head. Like she’s standing there, even when the house is quiet.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any unusual smells, tastes, or feelings on your skin that couldn't be explained by anything around you?\n\nPatient: Not really smells or tastes… no. It’s more the voice by the kitchen window, when it’s quiet. Feels like she’s right there.\n\nDoctor: Do you hear the woman's voice all the time or just sometimes?\n\nPatient: Just sometimes. Mostly when the house is quiet… I’ll hear her by the kitchen window, telling me to check the locks or move his pills. It feels real clear then.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. They’re checking if I’m taking care of my husband right.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people watching you are plotting something against you or is it just observation?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say “plotting.” They’re watching, though. The hospital people and some from the agency… they’re checking up on me, on the house and where I clean, to see if I’m taking care of my husband right.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever seen or heard these people from the hospital or agency when no one else is around?\n\nPatient: Sometimes… yeah. When it’s quiet at home, I hear a woman by the kitchen window. She’ll tell me to check the locks, or move my husband’s pills. It sounds like she’s right there.\n\nDoctor: Do you only hear the woman's voice or do you also see her?\n\nPatient: Mostly I hear her. I don’t really see her… it’s like she’s by the kitchen window, clear enough like she’s standing there, telling me to check the locks or move his pills.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voice you hear is coming from inside your head or from outside?\n\nPatient: It feels like it’s outside… near the kitchen window. Not in my head. Like she’s standing there, even when the house is quiet.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any unusual smells, tastes, or feelings on your skin that couldn't be explained by anything around you?\n\nPatient: Not really smells or tastes… no. It’s more the voice by the kitchen window, when it’s quiet. Feels like she’s right there.\n\nDoctor: Do you hear the woman's voice all the time or just sometimes?\n\nPatient: Just sometimes. Mostly when the house is quiet… I’ll hear her by the kitchen window, telling me to check the locks or move his pills. It feels real clear then.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to keep track of what you're saying when you're talking about the people watching you and the voice by the kitchen window?\n\nPatient: Sometimes, yes. I try to explain it and… it gets mixed up. I’ll start with my husband’s heart pills, then I’m talking about the church schedule, then numbers from my client list. People tell me they can’t follow what I’m saying.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your thoughts are organized and make sense when you're not hearing the voice?\n\nPatient: I think so… more than when I hear it. But when I try to explain what’s happening, it still gets tangled. I’ll go from my husband’s heart pills to the church schedule, then numbers from my client list, and people say they can’t follow me.\n\nDoctor: Do you find your thoughts are disorganized like that all the time, or just when you're talking about the voice or the people watching you?\n\nPatient: Mostly when I’m trying to explain it. I don’t mean to jump around, but the words go from my husband’s heart pills to the church schedule, then those numbers on my client list… and people say they can’t follow me.\n\nDoctor: Do you have days where your thoughts feel completely organized and it's easy to explain things to others?\n\nPatient: Some days are better, I guess. But when I try to explain what’s happening, it still runs together… the heart pills, the church schedule, the numbers from my client list. Then people say they can’t follow me.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your thoughts are disorganized even when you're not thinking about the voice or the people watching you?\n\nPatient: Not all the time… but it can happen when I’m trying to explain things. The words kind of run together—from my husband’s heart pills to the church schedule, then numbers on my client list—and people tell me they can’t follow what I’m saying.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any time when your thoughts feel completely clear and organized, without any confusion?\n\nPatient: Maybe sometimes, if I’m not trying to explain it. But when I do, it gets mixed up… like I start with my husband’s heart pills, then the church schedule, then the numbers from my client list. People say they can’t follow me.\n\nDoctor: Are your thoughts usually disorganized, or is it just when you're trying to explain what's happening with the voice and the people watching you?\n\nPatient: Mostly when I’m trying to explain it. I start in one place and then… it runs together—my husband’s heart pills, the church schedule, numbers from my client list. People say they can’t follow me.\n\nDoctor: Do you have times when your thoughts feel completely clear and organized without any confusion at all?\n\nPatient: Sometimes, I guess.
